**Book 167:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas VI* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dicky Susanto, Savitri Sihombing, Ambarsari Kusuma Wardani, Ummy Salmah, Eunice Salim, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-900-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 6 student book cited in this lesson — one guide section per student chapter with step-by-step teaching notes, per-subsection misconception warnings, full Latihan and Uji Kompetensi answer keys, alternative-teaching suggestions and technology links (printed pp. 1–154). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Chapter 4 answer key (printed p. 133) picks the right die for its item 4c but justifies it backwards — the pyramid die has fewer total outcomes (4 vs 6), which is why its chance of a 3 is larger; taught corrected. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 168:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SMP/MTs Kelas IX* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Yosep Dwi Kristanto, Muhammad Taqiyuddin, Elyda Yulfiana, Indra Rukmana, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-901-1. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 9 student book cited in this lesson — the Fase D learning-outcome flow for Grade 9, lesson schemes, teaching guidance per sub-section, technology integration (Desmos, GeoGebra, CODAP) and worked answer keys for every Latihan, Uji Kompetensi and Proyek of the student book (printed pp. 1–276). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its contents misprints the Bab III and Bab IV starts as pp. 147 and 207; the chapters open at printed pp. 146 and 206. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 169:** *Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SMA/MA/SMK/MAK Kelas X (Edisi Revisi)* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan, 2023, ISBN 978-623-118-562-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 10 Revisi student book cited in this lesson — Panduan Umum (printed pp. 1–28) plus one guide section per Revisi chapter (Bab 1 pp. 29–62; Bab 2 pp. 63–102; Bab 3 pp. 103–124; Bab 4 pp. 125–148; Bab 5 pp. 149–198; Bab 6 pp. 199–242; Bab 7 pp. 243–286) with objectives, achievement criteria, suggested lesson hours, prerequisite notes, teaching guidance and full answer keys. It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Negative evidence: the guide has no vectors chapter, and it handles the student book's Bab 1 logarithms enrichment only by an external link — it is not cited for vectors or logarithms teaching. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 170:** *Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SMA/MA/SMK/MAK Kelas XII (Edisi Revisi)* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Mohammad Tohir, Ahmad Choirul Anam, Ibnu Taufiq, Tista Imam Riyadi, Kementerian Pendidikan Dasar dan Menengah (Kemendikdasmen), 2025, ISBN 978-634-00-3116-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 12 Revisi student book cited in this lesson — Panduan Umum (printed pp. 1–44) plus four chapter guides (Bab 1 pp. 45–106; Bab 2 pp. 107–166; Bab 3 pp. 167–220; Bab 4 pp. 221–276) with objectives, prerequisites, chapter frameworks, formative assessment, differentiated teaching guidance, summative tasks with rubrics, full worked answer keys and reflections (printed pp. 1–300). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book; it carries no function-transformations chapter guide (the topic appears only in its Panduan Umum outcome tables) and is not cited for function transformations. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

| The normal distribution — the density f(x) = (1/(σ√(2π))) e^(−½((x−μ)/σ)²) and the notation X ~ N(μ, σ²); its shape and properties (symmetry, mean = median = mode; the 68.27 / 95.45 / 99.73 areas); standardisation Z = (X − μ)/σ; the standard normal φ(z), Φ(z) and its tables; probabilities, quantiles and an unknown μ or σ found for any normal distribution | Pakistan (Punjab), Statistics Class 12 (PCTB, 2016), Ch. 10, printed pp. 1–19 |
| Sampling distributions — of a statistic and its standard error; of the sample mean (μ_x̄ = μ, σ_x̄ = σ/√n, the finite population correction, and the Central Limit Theorem); of a difference of two means; of a proportion (the binomial model and its normal approximation; the hypergeometric model without replacement); of a difference of proportions; of the sample variance S² | Pakistan (Punjab), Statistics Class 12 (PCTB, 2016), Ch. 11, printed pp. 35–67 |
| Independence and association of attributes — the independence criterion (AB) = (A)(B)/n checked against observed frequencies; positive, negative, complete association and complete disassociation | Pakistan (Punjab), Statistics Class 12 (PCTB, 2016), Ch. 15, printed pp. 217–220 |
| The standard normal tables as printed apparatus — Table 7 (ordinates φ(z)), Table 9 (the CDF Φ(z)) and Table 10 (quantiles Φ⁻¹), their values spot-checked correct | Pakistan (Punjab), Statistics Class 12 (PCTB, 2016), Appendix B, printed pp. 289–297 |
| The outcomes of a random experiment are everything that can be obtained when it is performed; an event is one outcome, or a set of outcomes; the chance of an event occurring is its probability; when all outcomes are equally likely, P(event) = number of favourable outcomes ÷ total number of outcomes (worked: an odd number on a fair die numbered 1–6 is 3/6 = 1/2; a prime number is likewise 1/2); every probability lies between 0 and 1 inclusive, shown on a number line — 0 the impossible event, 1 the certain event, 1/2 an even chance; complementary events (الحدثان المتمّمان): the probabilities of an event and its complement add to 1; probability estimated from records — a train was late 5 times in 10 recorded days, so the probability it is late today is estimated as 1/2 | Syria, Grade 7 student's book, printed pp. 175–178 |
| Relative frequency as the entry idea (17 red balls out of 54 gives the relative frequency 17/54); random experiment and simple (elementary) events defined, the possible outcomes of an experiment being its simple events; the probability tree (شجرة الاحتمالات) introduced for a coin and for a die, with probabilities written on the branches, for a fair coin P(H) = P(T) = 1/2 and all simple events' probabilities summing to 1; two ways to find an event's probability: add the probabilities on the tree branches that realise it, or divide the number of realising outcomes by the total number of possible outcomes; unequal simple events from a spinner (a disc in 6 equal sectors numbered 1, 2, 3 gives P(1) = 1/3, P(2) = 1/6, P(3) = 1/2); the impossible event ∅ with P(∅) = 0 and the certain event Ω with P(Ω) = 1, every event satisfying 0 ≤ P(A) ≤ 1; relative frequency approaches the probability when an experiment is repeated many times; expected counts from probability (tossing a fair coin 1,000 times, the count of one face should be close to 500, not necessarily exactly 500); complementary events: P(A′) = 1 − P(A); mutually exclusive events (أحداث متنافية) — two events that cannot occur together — and the addition rule P(A or B) = P(A) + P(B); compound (two-stage) experiments on a two-level probability tree, with the path rule (the probability of an outcome at the end of a path is the product of the probabilities along it) and paths and complements combined (two draws with replacement from 3 blue and 2 green cards: P(same colour twice) = 0.36 + 0.16 = 0.52, so P(different colours) = 0.48) | Syria, Grade 9 Algebra student's book, printed pp. 95–106 |
| The sample space Ω of an experiment and its events; equally likely outcomes are an assumption read off the wording — 'ideal/unbiased die', 'balanced coin', 'chosen at random'; conditional probability (Definition 1): for P(B) > 0, P_B(A) = P(A ∩ B)/P(B), used in the product form P(A ∩ B) = P(B)·P_B(A); probability trees: the branches out of a node carry conditional probabilities summing to 1 (node rule), a complete path's probability is the product along it, and an event's probability is the sum of the paths leading to it; the law of total probability (Theorem 2): if Ω is the disjoint union of A₁, …, Aₙ then P(E) = Σᵢ P(Aᵢ)·P_{Aᵢ}(E); a Bayes-type question worked straight from the definition (having drawn black, the probability it came from the first of two boxes); probabilistically independent experiments — outcome probabilities multiply; a random variable (Definition 3) is a function defined on Ω taking real values (the book stresses it is a function, not a 'variable'), with its probability law (Definition 4) Σpᵢ = 1; expectation, variance and standard deviation (Definition 5): E(X) = Σ pᵢxᵢ, V(X) = Σ pᵢ(xᵢ − E(X))² with the equivalent computing form V(X) = E(X²) − (E(X))² proved, σ(X) = √V(X); the joint law of a pair (Definition 6) — the marginals recovered by summing rows or columns, the joint law not rebuildable from the marginals; independence of two random variables (Definition 7), tested entry by entry; Bernoulli trials and the binomial law (Definition 8): X follows B(n, p) with P(X = k) = C(n, k) p^k q^{n − k}, E(X) = np and V(X) = npq (Theorem 4); the complement used for 'at least' events | Syria, Grade 12 Scientific, Part Two, printed pp. 152–173 |
| Certain and impossible events (a spinner that cannot land on blue); more likely and less likely judged by counting the parts, then tested with a 10-draw tally experiment | Saudi Arabia, Grade 2, Part One, Ch. 4, printed pp. 100–103 |
| Probability words — certain, more likely, less likely, impossible — from ball bags, counters and a spinner (تأكيد: أكيد، أكثر احتمالاً، أقل احتمالاً، مستحيل) | Saudi Arabia, Grade 3, Part Two, Ch. 10, printed pp. 170–173 |

| Calculating the possibilities (Боломжийг тооцоолох): play the game by drawing one card, face down, from the following 2 sets of number cards (the girl's cards 1, 6, 5, 3, 8, 10 and the boy's cards 12, 6, 8, 2, 4, 15: a. from whose cards is the possibility of drawing an even-number card greater, if one is drawn?; b. from whose cards is the possibility of drawing smaller?; c. from whose cards is the possibility of drawing an odd-number card greater?; d. from whose cards is there a possibility of drawing a two-digit number card?); with the digits 1, 2, 3 and 4, how many improper fractions can be written; toss a coin: is the emblem (Сүлд) more likely to land than the number (Тоо), or are both equally possible — make an experiment of tossing one coin 15 times, record the result in the table and discuss the conclusion (the tally table of the 15 trials with the rows for emblem and number); choose a number from the following two sets (the ovals A and Б with their sixteen numbers each: a. from which set, if chosen, is the possibility of an even number greater?; b. from which set is the possibility of an odd number smaller?; c. from which set is the possibility of a number divisible by five greater?; d. a number divisible by both two and three is more possible from which set?); if one card is drawn from a pack of 32 cards, which possibility is greater: that the card is a diamond (гил), or that it is a card of another colour (the other colours, 24 cards against 8); a puzzle: in the first lottery, one of every 5 tickets is prize-winning, and in the next lottery one of every 100 tickets is prize-winning: in which lottery is the possibility of winning a prize greater if you take part — discuss (the first lottery); toss two coins at the same time: count in how many ways it is possible (the table of the 4 trials with the two coins' faces pictured: С for the emblem side and Т for the number side, giving TT, TS, ST and SS); register the record by the tree model (a boy's definitions: "Any phenomenon or experiment's result is called a possibility. The count of all the results is called the number of possibilities"; the tree diagram: from the first coin's Т branch two branches, Т and С, come out, and from its С branch also two branches, Т and С: the number of trees is 2 and the number of branches is 2, so the number of possibilities is 2 × 2 = 4); with the digits 2, 3 and 6, how many two-digit numbers can be written — let's calculate by the tree model (a. the digits are not repeated: the trees headed 6, 3 and 2 give 63, 62, 36, 32, 26, 23: the number of trees is 3, one tree's branches are 2, the total branches are 3 × 2 = 6; b. the digits are repeated: the trees give all nine numbers from 22 to 66: the number of trees is 3, the branches are 3, the total is 3 × 3 = 9); from Erdenet city to Bulgan aimag three different roads go, and from Bulgan to Khövsgöl aimag two different roads go: by how many different roads can one travel from Erdenet to Khövsgöl (3 × 2 = 6); with the digits 2, 3 and 1, how many two-digit numbers can be written (a. the digits are not repeated; b. the digits are repeated); with the digits 5, 8 and 0, how many two-digit numbers can be written (a. the digits are not repeated (4); b. the digits are repeated (6)); count how many different rectangles there are in the picture and register them in the table (the rectangle-counting figure and the table by size, the first count given as 6); a puzzle: into at most how many parts can a circle be divided by four lines (11 parts) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 94–95 |
| Calculating the possibilities (Боломжийг тооцоолох): make an experiment of tossing a die and a coin at the same time, once: count in how many different ways they can land (when the die is tossed it can land with 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 or 6 spots, and when the coin is tossed it can land emblem (С) or number (Т): both together are □ possibilities — the table of the die's six faces against the coin's С and Т rows, giving 12 outcomes); which is more: the possibilities of two dice's spots summing to 5, or of their summing to 11 (the sum 5 has 4 ways and the sum 11 has 2 ways)?; in how many different ways can a shagai (шагай, an ankle-bone die) and a coin, tossed at the same time, land (the shagai's 4 landing sides × the coin's 2 = 8)?; in how many different ways can two different shagai, tossed at the same time, land (4 × 4 = 16)?; an activity: make an experiment of tossing two different-coloured dice and answer the questions (a. how many possibilities are there of the two dice's spots summing to less than five?; b. how many possibilities of their summing to more than ten?; c. which of the two above cases is more possible?); in a bag there are 3 white and 2 red balls: guess and take the smallest number of balls that guarantees (a. one white ball (3 balls); b. one red ball (4 balls)); in a box there are red, blue, green and yellow balls, 4 balls in all: a. if two are taken at once without looking, what colours of balls can be matched? Write all the possibilities; b. if three balls are taken, what colours of balls can be matched?; find the most possible (a boy throws a stone at the following targets: say the number of the target whose shaded part the thrown stone has the most possibility of hitting — the three circle targets divided into eighths with different numbers of parts shaded); let's play a guessing game (Тааж тоглоё): two pupils in turn toss one die 10 times each and make an experiment: before tossing the die, predict beforehand each spot's count, and register the prediction in the table (the table with the die's faces, the two pupils' predictions (таамаглал), the frequency counts (давтамжийн тоо) and the total (нийт); the scoring rule rewards the prediction closest to the result: the pupil with the most points wins); make an experiment: toss two dice 10 times and register the landed positions in the following tables (the full grid of the 36 pairs of dice faces; a. mark in the table the product and the sum of the numbers of the spots of the two dice tossed, and carry out this action 10 times; b. of the products and the sums of the two dice's spots' numbers, how many are even numbers?); in a bag there are 3 white and 4 red balls (a. taking one ball without looking, what colour ball can it be?; b. taking two balls at once without looking, what colours of balls can they be?; c. taking three balls at once without looking, what colours of balls can they be? Write the possibilities); a puzzle: a. writing the numbers from 1 to 100 in a row, how many times does the digit 5 enter (20 times)?; b. with what digit do the products of the numbers up to 20 end?; c. what is the sum of the numbers up to 100 (5050)?; count the squares that can be formed by joining the points (the 4 × 4 dot grid: count the squares of side 1, 2 and 3 units, add each such square's counts, count the total number of possibilities: draw all 14 squares (9 + 4 + 1)); in how many ways can three red and two yellow balls be arranged in a row (У for red (улаан), Ш for yellow (шар): УУУШШ, УУШУШ, … — continue and complete in your notebook: 10 arrangements); by how many different roads can a boy go from home to school: draw them, continuing in your notebook (the pictured street grids with their partial routes); writing the numbers from 1 to 100, how many times does the digit 3 enter (20 times)?; three boys and three girls dance in pairs: how many are the possibilities (9)?; from Ulaanbaatar city to Darkhan city three different roads go, and from Darkhan city to Erdenet city four different roads go: by how many different roads can one go from Ulaanbaatar through Darkhan to Erdenet (12; the route diagram with the roads 1, 2, 3 and A, Б, В, Г); Dorj, Bat, Tsetseg and Alimaa race their horses: how many are the possibilities for their coming in 1st, 2nd and 3rd (4 × 3 × 2 = 24)?; mother gives 6 apples to her 3 children: in how many ways can she give them, if the children are given different numbers of apples (the splits of 6 into 1, 2 and 3 in their 6 orders)?; a puzzle: with the letters A, B, V, G, how many different words can be composed (the words composed may be meaningful or meaningless: 24) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 117–119 |
| Consolidation: chances compared; routes on a map; scale distances on a picture — three sellers sell tickets with a lottery: the first seller sold 20 tickets, the second seller 60 tickets and the third seller 40 tickets: from which seller's buyer is the chance the greater? Discuss; observe the picture of the positions: by how many different roads (маршрут) can a boy go from home to school (the map joining the home (гэр), the shop (Дэлгүүр), the library (Номын сан), the playground (Тоглоомын талбай), the teahouse (Цайны газар) and the school (Сургууль))?; calculate how many different triangles there are in the following shape (the big triangle cut by the lines from its corners); with the digits 5, 7, 1, 0 and 9 write the biggest and the smallest five-digit numbers (97510 and 10579); on the picture, 50 metres is drawn represented by one centimetre: answer the following questions (the houses of Bat, Bold, Bilguun and Nyam pictured along one road: a. at what distance does Bat's house stand from Nyam's?; b. at what distance does Bold's stand from Bilguun's?; c. at what distance does Bilguun's stand from Bat's? — measuring on the picture and multiplying by 50 m); fold a rectangular piece of paper equally and cut it in the position shown in the picture: discuss what shape the cut-out piece will be when it is unfolded and spread (the paper with its dashed zigzag cutting lines and the scissors); a puzzle: 1. if the digit 5 has entered 12 times in numbering a book's pages, how many pages has the book (55 pages)?; 2. if a book has 120 pages, how many digits have entered in numbering its pages (252)?; mother uses 100 grams of raisins to prepare 1 litre of raisin juice: by this method, how many kilograms of raisins are needed to make 20 litres of raisin juice (2 kg)?; using the ratio, find the real measure (the table of the ratios (Харьцаа) 1 : 2, 1 : 7, 1 : 2 and 1 : 2 against the pictured boot (the length on the picture (Зураг дээрх уртын хэмжээ) 11 cm), the green shape (3 cm), the pink shoes (9 cm 5 mm) and the blue shoes (10 cm 5 mm), the real measures (Бодит хэмжээ) to be filled); in a glass cup there was fruit juice up to the brim: Misheel drank 50 grams of it, and 3 parts of 4 of the juice remained in the glass cup (a. what part of the juice did Misheel drink (1/4)?; b. how many grams of juice were in the cup at first (200 g)?); tell the number of the picture drawn symmetrically with respect to the dashed line (the four triangles with their mirror halves drawn below the dashed lines); express the measure in the given unit (1/5 m = □ cm; 1/10 m = □ cm; 3/4 m = □ cm; 2/10 km = □ m; 0.4 m = □ cm; 4.5 kg = □ kg □ g; 0.5 t = □ kg; 5.4 l = □ l □ ml); write the suitable unit for the following things' measures (a. a notebook's thickness; b. a cup's water; c. a vegetable field's length; d. the road from Ulaanbaatar to Töv aimag); observe the tables, move the measure across, and write it in your notebook (the three conversion tables: of the tonnes, centners, kilograms and grams; of the kilometres, metres, decimetres, centimetres and millimetres; and of the years (жил), months (сар), days (хоног), hours (цаг) and minutes (минут)); when a big unit is changed into a small unit, express it in the form of a common fraction | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 145–146 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 65 countries (Egypt, Jordan, Greece, Sierra Leone, Libya, Vietnam, Mauritania, Armenia, Ghana, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, North Macedonia, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Malaysia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, Afghanistan, Ecuador, Albania, Turkey, South Africa, Kazakhstan, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Colombia, Moldova, Australia, Brazil, Bhutan, Costa Rica, Cuba, Eritrea, Chile, Finland, Dominica, Dominican Republic, Germany, Ireland, Fiji, Honduras, Italy, Iceland, Mozambique, Peru, Thailand, Uganda, Samoa, United States, Uruguay).**.** Egypt carries the full first course (sample spaces, theoretical and experimental probability, complements), a probability lesson in Prep 2's new Term 2 book (row stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep), and the distributions course (conditional probability, random variables, binomial, geometric and normal); Jordan carries counting outcomes, compound events, Venn and geometric probability, permutations, combinations, random variables and distributions; Greece carries probability from Grade 5's dice game through Grade 12's operations with events and conditional probability; Sierra Leone carries the addition and multiplication laws, outcome tables, tree diagrams and conditional probability without replacement; Libya carries probability theory with conditional probability and the distributions; Vietnam carries the probability rules, conditional probability and Bayes; Mauritania carries counting and probability through three streamed books; Armenia carries experiment, event and equally likely events; Ghana's evidence is teacher evidence only. Indonesia carries probability from the qualitative probability scale in Grade 6, through theoretical and empirical probability with sampling in Grade 9, to distributions and the addition and multiplication rules in Grade 10, counting rules with Pascal's triangle and compound-event probability in Grade 12, uniform, binomial and normal distributions in Grade 12 Tingkat Lanjut, and random variables with probability and density functions in the Grade 12 Tingkat Lanjut Revisi student book; Bayes' formula is not a named topic in the held books. Nicaragua carries probability from counting groundwork — arrangements and combinations in Grade 6 — through the full first course in Grade 10 (experiments, sample spaces, events, tree diagrams, counting, classical and frequentist probability) to counting re-taught with circular permutations and the full conditional-probability and independence teaching in Grade 11; its Grade 10 book states no addition rule as a formula and teaches no conditional probability or independence, and its printed p. 21 sample space is corrected to 16 quadruples in this lesson. Iraq carries probability from the certain/possible/impossible vocabulary in Grade 4 — no P(E) as a number at that grade — through experimental outcomes and comparing probabilities in Grade 7, to formal probability P(E) = m/n with complementary events and experimental versus theoretical probability in Grade 8, permutations, combinations and compound events in Grade 9, and the counting, probability-ratio and probability-laws course with the binomial theorem in Grade 11 Scientific. The Literary track never meets probability, and Iraq teaches no probability distributions anywhere — elementary probability only. Nepal carries probability only in part — elementary probability (meaning, simple events, the probability scale, empirical probability) in Grade 9 compulsory and the addition and multiplication laws with tree diagrams in Grade 10 compulsory; it teaches no permutations, combinations or distributions, the Optional books have no probability unit at all, and Grade 4 teaches no probability. Cambodia carries probability whole — experiments and the probability scale in Grade 7, coin tosses in Grade 8 and experimental frequency in Grade 9, with sample space, conditional probability, permutations and combinations, discrete random variables and the binomial distribution taught whole in the private Grade 12 Probability booklet and the normal distribution in the Grade 12 Advanced book; tree diagrams as a named method, geometric probability, Poisson and geometric distributions, continuous random variables and inference are not taught anywhere in the set. North Macedonia carries probability in Grade 8 (2010 edition) in both its forms — statistical probability from relative frequency, and mathematical probability as m/n for equally likely outcomes. Iran grows probability from spinner fractions in Grade 4 and chance language in Grade 5 to the favourable-over-total formula in Grade 7 and tree diagrams in Grade 8, and — in Grade 11 Statistics and Probability — to conditional probability, the multiplication rule, the law of total probability, Bayes' rule and independence. Its Grade 11 statistics book mentions hypothesis testing only as history (Fisher, printed p. 79) and does not teach it, so no such stock is claimed.

Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик IV, V, VI, VII, VIII, IX, X (Grades 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10): chance met first as calculated possibilities in Grade 4 — chances compared, dice and coins, and guaranteed draws that force a result — before probability is named in the middle years.

**What this lesson does not claim:** no read country teaches continuous distributions as a lesson of its own in the primary/secondary ladder — the normal distribution is taught descriptively and for applications (Egypt Grade 12, Jordan Grade 12, Greece Grade 12, Libya Grade 12), and the curriculum records continuous distributions as a topic with no lesson yet. This lesson names the normal distribution as the books teach it and goes no further. Laos carries probability whole in Grade 11 — random experiments and sample spaces, then random variables and their distributions with a binomial table. Yemen carries probability from the vocabulary — certain, impossible, more likely, less likely — in Grade 2, through a taught probability lesson in Grade 3, to sample spaces in Grade 11 science and the full probability unit in Grade 12 science. Ethiopia carries probability from predicting the outcomes of simple experiments in Grade 5, through the first probability course — sample space, events, equally likely outcomes and P = favourable ÷ total — in Grade 8 and experimental vs theoretical probability with tree diagrams for three coins in Grade 9, to counting, permutations and combinations, the binomial theorem and probability of an event in Grade 11; it teaches no probability distributions anywhere (no normal or binomial distribution as a topic — the binomial theorem is taught, not the binomial distribution), no combined events beyond three coins, and no expected value. South Sudan carries probability from the 0–1 scale with certain and impossible in Primary 7, through chance vocabulary with card and dice examples and probabilities as fractions in Primary 8, to experimental and theoretical probability, sample spaces, complementary events, grids and tree diagrams for two-stage events in Secondary 1 — with four corrections: Primary 7 lists "tomorrow is Friday if today is Monday" as uncertain, but its probability is 0; Primary 7 sets no probability calculations (no P(event) = favourable/total); Primary 8 pp. 89–90 end their examples without stating the probability — not a spade is 39/52 = ¾ and an odd sum of two dice is 18/36 = ½; Secondary 1 p. 120 Exercise 3 Q3(a) asks for the probability of "no first name" when the data accounts for all 32 students, so the answer is 0 and is treated with care. No probability distributions are taught anywhere in South Sudan's set. Kuwait carries probability from simple certain/possible/impossible work in Grade 5, through simple probability in Grade 6 and sample space and compound events in Grade 7, to counting methods with permutations and combinations in Grade 8. Band C adds odds and simple probability in Grade 9 Term 1, counting methods and conditional probability with Bayes' rule in Grade 10 Term 2, counting, the binomial theorem and probability in Grade 11 Term 2 in both tracks (the scientific student book, and the literary Statistics student book with its workbook), and — as the distributions thread only — random variables with the binomial and normal distributions in Grade 12 Term 2 in both tracks, with the two books' summary slips on interval probabilities taught corrected.

China carries probability whole in Grade 9 Volume 1 — the classical model, tree diagrams and frequency estimation — and in the senior A-edition Volume 2: sample spaces, event operations, independence, frequency stability and Monte Carlo simulation.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries probability from Class 6's first sample spaces through Classes 7–8's rules to the Class 11 statistics book's full probability, distributions and binomial/hypergeometric chapters and the Class 12 book's normal distribution and sampling distributions — the set's deepest statistics stock.

Syria introduces probability in Grade 7 — favourable over total, the 0-to-1 line, complementary events and estimates from records — builds trees, mutually exclusive events and two-stage experiments in Grade 9 Algebra, and completes the theory in Grade 12 Part Two: conditional probability, the law of total probability, random variables with expectation and variance, and the binomial law.

Saudi Arabia words probability carefully: certain/impossible and more/less likely in Grade 2 with a draw-and-tally experiment, the four terms again in Grade 3, and in Grade 4 the numerical form "k out of n" with the half-the-total judgement — witnessed by the older 1445 Term-3 volume.

Malaysia teaches probability in Form 2 from experiments to the complement rule and the simple problems that combine all three, then combined events in Form 4 — tree diagrams, the addition rule on Venn diagrams, and at-least-one problems.

Rwanda teaches probability from chance games in Primary 4, through the vocabulary of chance and recorded experiments in Primary 5–6, to the full Senior 1 course — sample space, experimental and theoretical probability and the complement rule — with tree diagrams in Advanced Senior Five and a Bayes' theorem evaluation question in Advanced Senior Six.

Sudan teaches probability in both Grade 12 tracks from the axioms: random experiment, sample space, events and their operations, the probability axioms and the addition rule in Basic; in Specialized Book One the same foundations, with the addition law named the book's “law of total probability”, as filed in the table.

India stocks probability from Mathematics Class 7–8, 10–12: chance language: certain, impossible, may-or-may-not events from everyday situations; first probability; theoretical probability; random experiments, outcomes and sample space.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Triángulos, rectángulos y algo más...* (Grade 2), *Aventuras de Patacalientes* (Grade 3), *La Patria Buena* (Grade 5), *Hecho en Venezuela* (Grade 6), *Matemática Para La Vida* (Grade 7), *Conciencia Matemática* (Grade 8), *La Matemática de la Belleza* (Grade 9), *Naturaleza Matemática* (Grade 10) and *La Matemática y el Vivir Bien* (Grade 11). From its books: from Grade 2: sure, possible, impossible — jar of 3 red + 2 white coconut sweets: events that are seguro, posible or imposible; from Grade 7: random experiments — Random vs deterministic experiments: an outcome that depends only on chance cannot be predicted beforehand (choosing a hallaca without tasting it); from Grade 11: the three probability axioms restated for the distribution.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 7: chance: certain, possible and impossible events in words; probability as a number between 0 and 1; experiencing an event: repeated coin-toss experiment (empirical probability); random experiment; sample space (result space) of an experiment; theoretical probability: favourable outcomes over total outcomes; chapter summary and exercises (probability); from Grade 8: relative frequency and probability; equally likely and unequally likely outcomes in a sample space; random events of a sample space (events as subsets; 2^n events); rules of probability: 0 ≤ P(E) ≤ 1, P(S) = 1, P(∅) = 0; chapter key points (probability); general exercises (probability); from Grade 11: the birthday problem: probability that k people share a birthday; from Grade 12: discrete and continuous sample spaces; equally likely events; multiplication principle for intersections; chapter key points and general exercises.

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 5 (teacher's book): Probability (first introduction, chance language and simple experiments). from Grade 6 (teacher's book): Chance language (certain, likely, fifty-fifty, unlikely, impossible), probability as wanted outcomes /…. from Grade 8 (teacher's book): .1 The language of probability (plan 81); .2 Outcomes (rezultate) of events (plan 82); .3 Calculating probability (plan 83). from Grade 9 (teacher's book): Calculating probabilities from tables; mutually exclusive events; probabilities of all outcomes sum to 1…; Probability (tree) diagrams for combined events; Independent events; two-way table of outcomes; p(BB) = 1/9 = (1/3)×(1/3) worked; with/without replacement…. from Grade 11 (teacher's book): Combined events; sample-space diagrams; counting outcomes (plan 2); Mutually exclusive events; probability of either event (plan 3; worked rule P(at least one red) + P(no red) =…; Independent events and tree diagrams; multiplying along branches, adding results (plan 5); The drug test: tree diagrams for test accuracy, comparing two projects' costs/probabilities (plan 8). from Grade 12 (teacher's book): Ngjarjet e papajtueshme dhe ngjarjet e pavarura (Mutually exclusive and independent events; deciding whether….

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 3 syllabus requires probability terms (the concept of probability), the probability of outcomes, and using probability to make predictions, all beginning at Class 3; Class 4 maintains the same programme. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Turkey enters through the pupil's textbooks of its Millî Eğitim Bakanlığı (MEB — Ministry of National Education), all in Turkish, first volumes (1. Kitap) only — Grade 3 (ISBN 978-975-11-9897-6, TTKB approval 13/07/2026), Grade 6 (ISBN 978-975-11-8773-4, TTKB approval 30.06.2025) and Grade 7 (ISBN 978-975-11-9899-0, TTKB approval 1.07.2026); no edition, printing or year line is printed in any of the three books, and the grades' second volumes (2. Kitap) are not in hand and are cited nowhere. From its books: from Grade 6: probability spectrum (olasılık spektrumu): judging everyday events on a 0 (impossible) to 1 (certain) line with 1/2 marked, before any formula; first experiment: drawing beads (3 red, 5 blue) with replacement, recording colours, comparing observed shares with spectrum estimates; core vocabulary defined on the coin toss: experiment (deney), outcome (çıktı), event (olay); expected vs observed frequency: predicting digit frequencies for 20 draws from cards 0–9, then running the experiment and comparing; relative frequency (göreli sıklık) defined: occurrences ÷ total trials, as a fraction and as a percentage; experimental probability (deneysel olasılık) defined as the relative frequency found from many repetitions; coin example 4/10 heads, 6/10 tails; experimental probability from real class data: blood groups of 20 pupils, frequency and relative-frequency tables, dot plot, percentage for group AB; worked examples: experimental probability as a fraction and as a percentage from dot plots, frequency tables and counts (coin 7 heads in 20; relative frequency 3/8 over 40 trials → frequency 15; white marble 4/20 → 20%); comparing experimental probabilities to choose: penalty takers 16/20 vs 22/30; defective drones 18/100 → sound 82%; target colours 3/15 red; inverse problems: completing a spinner table mixing frequencies and experimental probabilities; least total defective count from probabilities 1/8, 1/2, 1/4, 1/8 with equal samples; experimental probability in context sets: 20 days of temperatures (27 °C on 3 days → 15%); yellow ball from draws where only red and blue counts are given; basketball team 8 wins in 10 → 80%.

Armenia's stock in this lesson is widened by the full re-verification sweep of 2026-11-08, which re-read Armenia's maths books page by page and rebuilt the placements from the PDFs. Newly stocked from its Mathematics 6 (student); the Algebra and Elements of Mathematical Analysis 12 teacher guide, Semester 1 (teacher evidence; the student book is not held); Algebra 9; Algebra and Elements of Mathematical Analysis 10 (Arakelyan); the Grade 10 advanced-programme teacher guide, Semester 2 (teacher evidence): event and probability; axioms of probability theory and applications; classical probability; certain, impossible and random events; preliminary information on probability theory; classical definition of probability; independent and dependent events; introduction to probability: independent and dependent events.

## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

Into this lesson the placements stock 3 items (2 from student books, 1 teacher evidence) from Book S8, Book S13, Book T12.

**Book S8:** *Libro de texto, 6.° grado* (student textbook, Grade 6, MINED 2026-tree edition) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); 21,048,663 bytes, 196 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's own printed folios.

**Book S13:** *Matemática, Libro de texto, 2.° año de bachillerato* (student textbook, Grade 11; JICA file LT11_01) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); 11,070,921 bytes, 228 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's own printed folios.

**Book T12:** *Guía metodológica, 2.° año de bachillerato* (teacher guide, Grade 11) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); held as two files (SM11_01: 11,032,829 bytes, 292 PDF pp.; SM11_02: 7,146,275 bytes, 260 PDF pp.). Cited by unit. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide reproduces the student book's lessons with lesson-by-lesson teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test (*Prueba de la unidad*) per unit; it is cited as teacher evidence, never as a pupil textbook.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Probability (chance as a fraction of equally likely outcomes) (Grade 6 student book, Unidad 11, Lección 2) | El Salvador — *Libro de texto, 6.° grado* (student textbook), Grade 6, printed p. 179 |

| TEACHER EVIDENCE: guide apparatus — lesson plans, worked solutions and the unit test for Unidad 8, probability (Grade 11 teacher guide) | El Salvador — *Guía metodológica, 2.° año de bachillerato* (teacher guide) — teacher evidence, Unidad 8 |

El Salvador is added to the countries line and to the acceptance count above on its student-textbook rows; where this lesson also carries El Salvador guide rows, they are teacher evidence only. El Salvador's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand Maths book (all 43 distinct works) has been read and stocked, and no link-only Maths book stands between it and closure (the 7 JICA exercise workbooks CE3–CE9 were never filed into the library and are outside the set).

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

Into this lesson the placements stock 2 items (2 from pupil books, 0 teacher evidence) from Book S13, Book S22.

**Book S13:** *Μαθηματικά Γ΄ Δημοτικού, Μέρος 4* (Mathematics, Grade 3 pupil's book, Part 4 — Units 6–7) — Cyprus, Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), Παιδαγωγικό Ινστιτούτο (Pedagogical Institute); 13,146,323 bytes, 116 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed folios (printed folio = PDF page throughout).

**Book S22:** *Μαθηματικά Στ΄ Δημοτικού, Μέρος 3* (Mathematics, Grade 6 pupil's book, Part 3 — Units 8–10) — Cyprus, Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), Παιδαγωγικό Ινστιτούτο (Pedagogical Institute); 20,973,128 bytes, 117 PDF pp. The file's first page carries a “01/07/2026” production stamp — not an edition date. Pages cited are PDF pages, as the read cites them.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

## Colombia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Colombia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **27 distinct works: 26 student books and 1 teacher guide, all Colombia Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN), in Spanish** — Escuela Nueva Matemáticas cartillas Grades 1–5 (11), Nivelemos Matemáticas student workbooks Grades 1–4 (4), Aulas sin fronteras Grade 6 Unidad 1 student book and its teacher guide (2), Postprimaria student guides Grades 6–9 (4), Secundaria Activa student modules Grades 6–9 (4), and Vamos a Aprender Matemáticas 10 and 11 (2) — 3,928 PDF pages — and placed 293 teaching items: 288 from the student books and 5 teacher-evidence items from the one teacher guide (Book 23). SET CORRECTIONS carried exactly: (1) the expansion map's “28 student + 1 teacher = 29” over-counts by 2 — textbooks.tsv carries duplicate records of Escuela Nueva Grado 1 Cartilla 1 and Grado 2 Cartilla 1 (old blob/main rows for the same two works that also landed as release assets); the distinct set is 27 works and the duplicates are cited once (the library records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records); (2) the set in hand contains NO Grade 3 Cartilla 3 — Grade 3 holds Cartillas 1–2 plus the Nivelemos Grade 3 workbook, and Cartilla 2 completes Unidades 6–9; nothing in hand is a mislabelled Cartilla 3, and no never-published ruling is needed or made; (3) Nivelemos Matemáticas Grado 1 (Book 10) has NO asset in any GitHub release — it was read from the MEN source file itself, landed at exactly the census size (5,020,415 bytes) with EOF intact; the library side still owes that file a GitHub landing. The two Vamos a Aprender books (Books 26–27) are image-only viewer assemblies with no text layer, read from rendered pages (covers, printed contents pp. 6–7 and body spot-checks — Matemáticas 10 printed p. 110 “Teorema del seno”, Matemáticas 11 printed p. 120 “Derivada de una función en un punto”); printed page = PDF page in the sampled range. In the sections below the books are cited as Books 1–27 in the placements' order. Colombia COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-book rows; Book 23's rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guide companions Book 15 with teaching plans, activity scripts and answers and teaches no student content itself — cited as the evidence kind it is and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Book errors: none material at placement grain — the Secundaria Activa Grade 9 module's contents page misprints two of its own headings (“Captítulo”, “Undiad 2”); the content is unaffected and rows use the corrected unit names. The placements' named exclusions — presentation letters, “how this book works” pages, author/imprint pages, transversal civics/health/environment themes, practice/evaluation apparatus (“Practica más”, “Evaluación del aprendizaje”, answer keys) and the Escuela Nueva “Estándares/Competencias” boxes at each unidad opening (MEN's own curriculum-standard statements; the taught content is placed at the guías themselves) — are stocked nowhere. The Círculos de Aprendizaje integrated books and the Retos para Gigantes Transición weekly guides are NOT in this set (filed as integrado, not matematicas) and are stocked nowhere. Colombia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand Maths book (27/27) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

Into this lesson the placements stock 10 items from Book 18, Book 19, Book 21, Book 22, Book 24, Book 25, Book 26, Book 27.

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 55 teacher-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3, Book 4.

**Book 1:** *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 1–6) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 376/2021, Annex VII; ISBN 978-99920-0-934-5; 688,455 bytes, 49 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 7–10) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 355/2023 (BOPA Núm. 91, 19-7-2023); ISBN 978-99920-0-847-2; 1,808,872 bytes, 56 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 3:** *Programa de matemàtiques aplicades a les ciències socials del batxillerat general* (curriculum programme, Grades 11–12) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 374/2023 (BOPA Núm. 91, 19-7-2023); 1,696,001 bytes, 41 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

## Moldova added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Moldova's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **12 books, all pupil textbooks: the Matematică / Mathematics student manual for each of Grades 1–12 (Republic of Moldova, in Romanian). There is no teacher guide and no syllabus in the Moldova Maths set in hand, so every Moldova row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Moldova counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 394 teaching items. The expansion map's “12 student + 0 teacher” is correct: textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files reconcile exactly at 12 distinct works — Grades 3–12 (10 books) recorded in books.json, Grades 1–2 as git-blob files recorded in textbooks.tsv. The old closed-52 inventory's “Moldova link-only, 0 in hand” is refuted: all 12 books sit secured in the tensae-code/liqlearns-textbooks GitHub repo and were read from there for this expansion (Grades 3, 4 and 6–12 via the mid-size-books-8 release; Grades 1, 2 and 5 via the Contents API on their textbooks.tsv git-blob paths), byte-verified — MD5s match books.json exactly for the ten Grades 3–12 books. The Grades 10–12 books are dual-track (profil real / profil umanistic) throughout; each section is stocked once, as one Moldova row, not once per track. Book errors: none found that changes a placement; the Grade 9 file's PDF p. 178 carries a stray page from another book (a biology text page) bound after the back cover in the publisher's print file — a file artifact, not part of the mathematics manual, cited nowhere. The placements' named exclusions — the “Cine seamănă culege” review blocks, the Recapitulare / sistematizare / evaluare sections, the “Să recapitulăm” summaries, the recap exercise sets and “Test sumativ” pages closing chapters, and the “Răspunsuri (și indicații)” answer keys — are stocked nowhere, except where a review section is a topic's only statement, which is then marked as such. Printed-page bases: Book 6 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Book 9 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Book 12 — printed pages as listed in the book's own Cuprins. Into this lesson the placements stock 6 items (all from pupil textbooks) from Book 6, Book 9 and Book 12.

**Book 6:** *Matematică: Manual pentru clasa a VI-a* (pupil textbook, Grade 6) — Moldova, MECC; curriculum Order nr. 906/2019; approved Order nr. 1343 din 03 decembrie 2020; 6,364,545 bytes, 246 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1).

**Book 9:** *Matematică: Manual pentru clasa a IX-a* (pupil textbook, Grade 9) — Moldova, MECC; 15,508,619 bytes, 178 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1). File artifact: the PDF's p. 178 carries a stray page from another book (a biology text page, “Ameliorarea organismelor. Biotehnologii”) bound after the back cover in the publisher's print file; it is not part of the mathematics manual and is cited nowhere.

**Book 12:** *Matematică: Manual pentru clasa a XII-a* (pupil textbook, Grade 12) — Moldova, MECC; 20,690,648 bytes, 266 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed pages as listed in the book's own Cuprins).

## Bahamas added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read the Bahamas' whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them Bahamas Ministry of Education curriculum or examination-syllabus documents; 0 pupil (student) textbooks and 0 teacher guides** — and placed 496 requirement items. A curriculum states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Bahamas item in this section is therefore CURRICULUM EVIDENCE — a statement of what the Bahamas requires, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is (Book 3's items are examination-SYLLABUS evidence, on the same standing); the Bahamas is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent, until a Bahamas pupil book is in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded the in-hand set as “4 student + 0 teacher” — the count of 4 is right, the kind is wrong; all four documents are ministry requirement documents (kind = curriculum in both the staging books.json files and textbooks.tsv). The file the records title *BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9* is actually the *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* — a 10-page scanned examination syllabus with no text layer, read visually page by page — and the held Senior High file is the July 2019 revision of the Grades 10–12 guidelines; both are placed as what they are. No pupil textbook or teacher guide is in hand: the pupil books Bahamas schools use are commercial (Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* Books 1–6 — existence verified in the records, no file held; Books 1–2 name Silver Burdett Ginn Mathematics and Harcourt Math in classroom use, and Book 4's bibliography names STP Caribbean Mathematics, Mathematics: A Complete Course, Fundamental Mathematics for the Caribbean and Nelson Caribbean Mathematics — named only, none held), and the one school-compiled pupil text with an exact link in the records (Akhepran International Academy, *Mathematics BJC Textbook — Grade 7*, 2023) was never downloaded and sits in no record set, so it is not part of this read and is cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered objective in each guidelines book's Scope of Work body — the Content column read with every objective — Book 3 at one item per numbered topic row of its Topics table and Book 4 at one item per CONCEPTS/TOPICS block; an objective restated in full on a continuation page is counted once, at its first full statement, and the two requirements the documents restate inside a second strand (Book 1 Grade 2 estimation, item 69; Book 2 Grade 5 rounding of decimals, item 256) are stocked as the placements place them, each row naming the restatement. The placements' named exclusions — Benchmarks, Scope-and-Sequence charts, pacing guides and Essential Questions in Books 1–2; the Problems of the Day sets, Materials Lists, assessment rubrics, the Children's Literature list and References (record items R1–R7 and R9–R11); Book 3's examination administration, its 19 general aims and its erratum sheet (R8); Book 4's Scope and Sequence charts, Glossary and Technology Integration pages — are stocked nowhere. Book errors found in the read are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 16 requirement-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3 and Book 4.

**Book 1:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 1–3* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,558,181 bytes, 263 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 51. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 4–6* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,278,639 bytes, 328 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 54. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 3:** *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* (examination syllabus) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Youth & Sports, Testing & Evaluation Section; 2,351,047 bytes, 10 PDF pages (a scanned-image PDF with no text layer, read visually page by page); printed page = PDF page − 1. The library records title this file “BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9” — the held file is the 2003 examination syllabus, and it is placed as what it is (set correction, stated here; the records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records). SYLLABUS EVIDENCE ONLY — an examination syllabus states what candidates are required to know; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 4:** *Senior High School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 10–12* (curriculum guidelines; the held file is the July 2019 revision, footer “High School Mathematics Curriculum – The Bahamas, 2019”) — Bahamas, Department of Education; 3,766,723 bytes, 219 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

The Bahamas enters this lesson as curriculum evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — every document in the Bahamas' in-hand set is a ministry requirement document (the 2010 primary guidelines, the 2003 BJC examination syllabus, the 2019 senior-high guidelines), not a pupil textbook or a teacher guide, cited as the evidence kind it is, never counted as teaching (the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent). The Bahamas' class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 4 in-hand documents are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand; it advances only when a pupil book file — a Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* volume, or the Akhepran Grade 7 BJC textbook whose exact link already sits in the download list — lands in hand and is read.

## Belize added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Belize's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **3 documents, all of them Belize Ministry of Education *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide* books (August 2012) — Infant 1, Standard 1 and Standard IV; 0 pupil (student) textbooks** — and placed 108 teaching items. Every document in the set is a teacher's guide: it states the learning outcomes pupils must reach and teaches the teacher how to teach each one (comments, sample lesson objectives, specific content, sample activities and assessment). Every Belize item in this section is therefore TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE — Belize's guides teach teachers how to teach the topic; no Belize pupil textbook is in hand — cited as the evidence kind it is; Belize is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until a Belize pupil book is in hand and read. No pupil Maths book is in hand: the pupil books on the official National Textbook Program list (*My BZ Math* Infant 1–Standard 4, Macmillan; *Caribbean Primary Math* Bk 6 for Standard 5, Hodder; *Active Math* for Standard 6, Macmillan) are all recorded missing in the library files, and the guides' own textbook linkages point to the legacy pupil series *Primary Mathematics for Belize* (Hodder, by term codes Y1T1…/S1T1…), which is named by the guides only — not in hand, not read, and not a course source. Three further Maths documents — the *Primary curriculum: Mathematics* division webs (Lower Division, Middle Division, Upper Division) — are recorded at exact link only, never downloaded and in no books.json; they are not part of this read and are cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered learning outcome in each guide's Standards and Learning Outcomes section (35 outcomes in each guide, 105 in all) plus 3 items taught in a detailed section but stated in no outcome of that guide (items 64, 65 and 87, marked D); outcome numbering is continuous across the series (Infant 1 carries M1.1–M20.1, Standard 1 continues at M1.10, Standard IV at M1.16), which is why the codes do not restart at 1 in the later guides. Printed page = PDF page in all three guides. The placements' named exclusions — the guides' front matter (programme philosophy: goals, rationale, assessment discussion), strand introductions and Big Ideas prose, sample lesson objectives, sample assessments, sample activities and print/technology resource columns as apparatus, the shared manipulatives list, the websites lists, the shared Mathematics Glossary (carried in the placements as record item R2 — a teacher reference list, cited nowhere as content), and the standards each guide marks N/A at its level (Book 1: M2 place value and M13 equations; Book 2: M14 measurement concepts; Book 3: M5 position/ranking numbers and M14) — are stocked nowhere. The 14 book errors and inconsistencies found in the read (placements E1–E14) are carried corrected on the rows they touch: item 35 is placed as data interpretation, not probability, though the guide files it under its probability standard (E5); item 51's set language is taught with “complement” for the book's “compliment” (E6); item 66's many-to-one correspondence is taught as one symbol or unit standing for many objects, not the guide's function-style definition (E8); item 82 is stocked WITHOUT the outcome's fourth bullet (“expand powers with same base to add or subtract”), which is not a valid add/subtract procedure (E9); item 81 is placed as stated and flagged as vague and advanced for its level (E10); item 104 is placed as measurement and taught with the standard measurement meanings, the guide's own definition of precision being a data-management sense (E13); and item 106 follows the guide's own correct definitions — its assessment text's “medium” for median is an error (E12). Into this lesson the placements stock 3 teacher-guide-evidence items from Book 2 and Book 3.

**Book 2:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard 1* (teacher guide, Grade 3) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 803,827 bytes, 62 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–16; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 17–56. Printed page = PDF page. Outcome numbering continues the series from Book 1 (M1.10 onwards). TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

**Book 3:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard IV* (teacher guide, Grade 6) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 868,323 bytes, 51 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–15; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 16–45. Printed page = PDF page. Outcome numbering continues the series (M1.16 onwards). The detailed sections of this guide are markedly thinner than in Books 1–2 (several state “Specific Content: N/A”); placements rest on the outcomes as stated. TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

Belize's class for Maths is PARTIAL (teacher-evidence-only): all 3 in-hand guides are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand, and the three *Primary curriculum: Mathematics* webs (Lower, Middle and Upper Division) sit at exact link only, unread.

## Australia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Australia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **9 books, all of them student textbooks** (Years 8–10: eight Cambridge volumes — the Essential Mathematics Australian Curriculum, Victorian Curriculum and CambridgeMATHS NSW lines, plus the GOLD support edition and the CORE pathway volume — and one Jacaranda volume, Maths Quest 10+10A) — and placed 844 teaching items. Australia has no government textbook series (ACARA writes the Australian Curriculum but publishes and prescribes no textbooks); the set is the school-used commercial series the country records name, and every book is cited as such. All 9 files are distinct works (distinct ISBNs, extents and contents, verified per book on the read); the publisher-line relations are stated in the placements: Book A1 (GOLD) is the support edition of Book A2's Year 8 material, Book A3 is the same line in its Victorian ©2024 edition, Books A4 and A6 are the NSW syllabus editions, and Book A9 is Book A8's CORE pathway companion. In the sections below the books are cited as Books A1–A9. Australia COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure: every item is pupil-textbook teaching. The read's four flagged claims are carried exactly: (F1) Book A3's contents lists its Chapter 11, Algorithmic thinking, as “(online only)”, but the file in hand contains the full chapter, bound after the Answers (printed pp. 926–941) — it is read and placed, and it founds this course's algorithmic-thinking lesson; (F2) Book A7's contents lists a Chapter 18, Algorithmic thinking, with no page numbers, and the chapter is NOT in the file (searched end to end; the book closes with its Glossary, printed p. 1106, and Index, printed p. 1117) — recorded as absent, not read, and no item rests on it; (F3) Book A9's running head at printed p. 644 prints section 10E's code as “0E” — the book's own production error; the placement cites 10E, as the section's heading and contents give it; (F4) only the 9 Maths books are this read's set — the country records' wider Australia picture (12 staged books across subjects, plus filled-by-exact-link titles) is not merged into this count, and in particular the two senior Maths titles that exist only as exact-link records (Cambridge Senior Specialist Mathematics, QCE Units 3&4, 2e, and Cambridge Senior Mathematical Methods, QCE Units 3&4, 2e) are NOT in hand and were NOT read. The placements' named exclusions — front matter and “how to use this book” guides; every chapter's pre-tests, progress quizzes, unlettered applications / problem-solving / modelling / investigation spreads, puzzles and games, chapter summaries, checklists and reviews, semester reviews, glossaries, indexes and answer sections — are apparatus/assessment, teach no new content, and are stocked nowhere. The set is Years 8–10 secondary only, so the primary lessons (counting, place value, first steps) receive no Australia item — that is the set's shape, not a reading gap. Australia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (9/9) has been read and placed; the two senior QCE exact-link titles do not block the in-hand class (if exact-link titles are ever ruled in-scope for the courses reckoning, Australia drops to PARTIAL until they are grabbed and read). Into this lesson the placements stock 31 items from Book A1, Book A2, Book A3, Book A5, Book A6, Book A7, Book A8, Book A9.

**Book A1:** *Cambridge Mathematics 8 (CambridgeMaths GOLD)* — Australia, Greenwood et al., Cambridge University Press, ©2012, ISBN 978-1-107-64383-3; 62,774,078 bytes, 554 PDF pp. The GOLD support edition of the Essential Mathematics Year 8 line, developed for students who require additional support in mathematics; a distinct work from Book A2 (different ISBN, extent and arrangement), not a duplicate file. Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 14).
